===============================================================================================
CASE #18: BAKING V. DIRECTOR OF PRISONS
Citation: G.R. No. L-30364 | July 28, 1969 | 28 SCRA 850 | En Banc | Ponente: J. Fernando
===============================================================================================

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LAYER A: ALAC DIGEST, RECITATION SCRIPT & STATCON ANALYSIS
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I. PARTIES & PROCEDURAL BACKGROUND
* Plaintiff/Petitioner: Baking (Petitioner)
* Defendant/Respondent: Director of Prisons (Respondent)
* Originating Court: Supreme Court En Banc (Original Action for Habeas Corpus)
* Nature of the Action: Petition for Habeas Corpus seeking full credit for preventive imprisonment under Article 29 of the Revised Penal Code.

II. WHOLE FACTS
Petitioner Baking was detained preventively during the trial of his criminal case before being convicted and sentenced to prison. He petitioned for habeas corpus, demanding that his entire period of preventive detention be credited in full against his prison sentence. Under Article 29 of the Revised Penal Code as originally enacted, detention prisoners were credited with only ONE-HALF (1/2) of their preventive imprisonment, provided they agreed to abide by the same disciplinary rules as convicted prisoners. Baking argued that under modern human rights standards, constitutional due process, and the Title of Article 29 ('Period of preventive imprisonment deducted'), full credit should be granted.

Plain Meaning Summary of Facts:
A prisoner who spent years in jail while waiting for his trial to finish demanded that 100% of his waiting time be deducted from his final prison sentence. Under the old Article 29 of the Revised Penal Code, the law strictly stated that detention prisoners only get 50% credit (half-time) for preventive detention. The Supreme Court ruled that while human rights principles are important, the written statute clearly and strictly allowed only one-half credit, and the courts cannot rewrite the law (Dura Lex Sed Lex). (Note: Congress subsequently amended Article 29 under RA 6127 to grant full credit).

III. THE LEGAL ISSUE
Whether or not a prisoner is entitled to full deduction of preventive imprisonment under the original text of Article 29 of the Revised Penal Code, testing Verba Legis and Dura Lex Sed Lex.

[HOW TO TACKLE IN RECITATION: THE LEGAL ISSUE]
* Plain Meaning: Can the Supreme Court grant a prisoner 100% credit for pre-trial jail time when the written penal statute strictly says 'one-half'?
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the issue is whether courts can expand the statutory credit for preventive imprisonment beyond the 'one-half' specified in Article 29 RPC, testing Verba Legis (the words of the law) and Dura Lex Sed Lex (the law is harsh, but it is the law)."

IV. ALAC DIGEST

[A] ANSWER:
NO. Under the clear text of Article 29 of the RPC as then written, detention prisoners were entitled to deduction of ONLY ONE-HALF (1/2) of their preventive imprisonment. Courts must apply the law as written (Dura lex, sed lex), leaving policy reforms to Congress.

* Plain Meaning: No. The text of the law explicitly said 'one-half'. Even if the rule was harsh and unfair, judges cannot change the math written in the statute. The duty of the court is to apply the law as written.
* Recitation Script: "No, Sir/Ma'am. The Supreme Court held that under Verba Legis and Dura lex, sed lex (the law is harsh, but it is the law), the explicit statutory fraction 'one-half' in Article 29 RPC was mandatory. The Court cannot engage in judicial legislation to grant full credit, which was subsequently enacted by Congress in RA 6127."

[L] LEGAL BASIS:
* 1. Article 29, Revised Penal Code (prior to amendment by R.A. No. 6127): Explicitly provided that offenders who have undergone preventive imprisonment shall be credited in the service of their sentence with one-half of the time.
* 2. Verba Legis Non Est Recedendum (from the words of the statute there should be no departure): When the statutory fraction is explicit, courts cannot substitute a different number.
* 3. Dura Lex, Sed Lex (the law is harsh, but it is the law): The harshness of a statutory deduction rule does not justify judicial modification.
* Plain Meaning: Article 29 RPC, Verba Legis, and Dura Lex Sed Lex.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the legal basis is Article 29 RPC, applied strictly under Verba legis non est recedendum (from statutory words there must be no departure) and Dura lex, sed lex (the law is harsh, but it is the law)."

[A] ANALYSIS / APPLICATION:
* 1. Plain Statutory Text Controls: Article 29 explicitly fixed the credit at 'one-half'. The language was unambiguous and left zero room for judicial discretion.
* 2. Prohibition Against Judicial Legislation: Expanding statutory credit from one-half to full credit is an exclusive prerogative of the Legislature. The judiciary cannot amend statutes under the guise of equitable construction.
* 3. Prompting Legislative Reform: The Supreme Court recognized the moral and humanitarian merit of full credit, which prompted Congress shortly thereafter to enact Republic Act No. 6127 amending Article 29 to provide full credit.
* Plain Meaning: The law said 'one-half'. You cannot read 'one-half' and pretend it says 'full credit'. Even though the Justices sympathized with the prisoner, they followed their judicial duty to apply the statute as enacted, while urging Congress to amend the law (which Congress did in RA 6127).
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the Court applied Dura Lex Sed Lex. Although the one-half credit rule was harsh, the text of Article 29 RPC was unambiguous under Verba Legis. The Court held that changing the fraction required an act of Congress, which led to the enactment of RA 6127."

[C] CONCLUSION:
The Supreme Court DENIED the petition for habeas corpus, HOLDING that petitioner was entitled only to one-half credit under Article 29 of the Revised Penal Code.

* Plain Meaning: The petition was denied and only half-time credit was granted under the existing statute.
* Recitation Script: "The Supreme Court denied the habeas corpus petition, applying the strict one-half credit under Verba Legis and Dura Lex Sed Lex."

V. RELATION TO STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION
* Syllabus Topic Alignment:
  IV. Aids to Construction -> A. Intrinsic Aids -> f. Lingual Text (Spanish Enacted Text vs English Translation / Complex Crimes)

* Governing Latin Maxims with English Translations:
  1. Dura lex, sed lex (the law is harsh, but it is the law)
     - Literal Translation: The law is harsh, but it is the law.
     - StatCon Application: The explicit statutory fraction 'one-half' in Article 29 RPC must be applied as written.
  2. Verba legis non est recedendum (from the words of the statute there should be no departure)
     - Literal Translation: From the words of the statute there must be no departure.
     - StatCon Application: Courts cannot change explicit numerical quantities in a penal statute.
  3. Judicis est ius dicere, non dare (it is the duty of a judge to declare the law, not to make it)
     - Literal Translation: It is the duty of a judge to state the law, not to create it.
     - StatCon Application: Changing preventive imprisonment credit requires legislative enactment, not judicial decree.

* Case Violation in StatCon: The petitioner sought to have the judiciary rewrite a clear statutory fraction from 'one-half' to 'full credit' in violation of separation of powers.
* Doctrinal Execution: Judicial Restraint on Explicit Statutory Quantities: Where the statute specifies an exact numerical ratio or fraction, courts must enforce it strictly under Dura Lex Sed Lex; humanitarian adjustments belong exclusively to the legislature.

* Plain StatCon Meaning: Judges cannot change numbers in a law; if the statute says 'half', judges must apply 'half' until Congress passes a new law.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, Baking v. Director of Prisons is a profound example of Dura lex, sed lex and Judicis est ius dicere non dare: courts cannot alter explicit statutory fractions."

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LAYER B: STANDARDIZED CASE DIGEST (STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION FORMAT)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Case Title: Baking v. Director of Prisons
G.R. No.:   G.R. No. L-30364
Date:       July 28, 1969
Ponente:    En Banc

I. FACTS OF THE CASE
Petitioner Baking was detained preventively during the trial of his criminal case before being convicted and sentenced to prison. He petitioned for habeas corpus, demanding that his entire period of preventive detention be credited in full against his prison sentence. Under Article 29 of the Revised Penal Code as originally enacted, detention prisoners were credited with only ONE-HALF (1/2) of their preventive imprisonment, provided they agreed to abide by the same disciplinary rules as convicted prisoners. Baking argued that under modern human rights standards, constitutional due process, and the Title of Article 29 ('Period of preventive imprisonment deducted'), full credit should be granted.

II. FACTS RELEVANT TO STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION
* Provision Relevant: 1. Article 29, Revised Penal Code (prior to amendment by R.A. No. 6127): Explicitly provided that offenders who have undergone preventive imprisonment shall be credited in the service of their sentence with one-half of the time.
2. Verba Legis Non Est Recedendum (from the words of the statute there should be no departure): When the statutory fraction is explicit, courts cannot substitute a different number.
* Circumstance Making Construction Necessary: The petitioner sought to have the judiciary rewrite a clear statutory fraction from 'one-half' to 'full credit' in violation of separation of powers.
* Competing Interpretations: Petitioner argued for a favorable interpretation based on statutory intent, while Respondent contended for a literal or restrictive application.

III. ISSUES
A. General Legal Issues: Whether or not a prisoner is entitled to full deduction of preventive imprisonment under the original text of Article 29 of the Revised Penal Code, testing Verba Legis and Dura Lex Sed Lex.
B. Statutory-Construction Issue/s: Whether the subject statutory act, classification, or terminology under the governing statute/rule means, includes, or applies to the factual controversy in Baking v. Director of Prisons, under the canon of Dura lex, sed lex.

IV. PROVISION/S OF LAW CONSTRUED
* Provision: 1. Article 29, Revised Penal Code (prior to amendment by R.A. No. 6127): Explicitly provided that offenders who have undergone preventive imprisonment shall be credited in the service of their sentence with one-half of the time.
2. Verba Legis Non Est Recedendum (from the words of the statute there should be no departure): When the statutory fraction is explicit, courts cannot substitute a different number.
* Interpretive Problem: Determining the precise legislative intent and boundary of the statutory terms in light of: The petitioner sought to have the judiciary rewrite a clear statutory fraction from 'one-half' to 'full credit' in viola...

V. RULING OF THE SUPREME COURT
A. General Ruling: The Supreme Court DENIED the petition for habeas corpus, HOLDING that petitioner was entitled only to one-half credit under Article 29 of the Revised Penal Code.
B. Ruling on Statutory-Construction Issue: NO. Under the clear text of Article 29 of the RPC as then written, detention prisoners were entitled to deduction of ONLY ONE-HALF (1/2) of their preventive imprisonment. Courts must apply the law as written (Dura lex, sed lex), leaving policy reforms to Congress.

VI. HOW THE COURT CONSTRUED THE LAW
A. Why Was Construction Necessary? Ambiguity in the application and scope of the statute: The petitioner sought to have the judiciary rewrite a clear statutory fraction from 'one-half' to 'full credit' in violation of separation o.
B. Purpose of Construction: To ascertain and give full effect to the true intent, spirit, and purpose of the lawmaking body.
C. Method/Canon Applied: Dura lex, sed lex
   - How Applied: Judicial Restraint on Explicit Statutory Quantities: Where the statute specifies an exact numerical ratio or fraction, courts must enforce it strictly under Dura Lex Sed Lex; humanitarian adjustments belong exclusively to the legislature.
   - Result: No. The text of the law explicitly said 'one-half'. Even if the rule was harsh and unfair, judges cannot change the math written in the statute. The duty of the court is to apply the law as written.
D. Other Sources Used: Ordinary meaning of words, statute considered as a whole, legislative history, and established Supreme Court jurisprudence.

VII. RELEVANT STATUTORY-CONSTRUCTION CONCEPTS (A–L EVALUATION)
* A. Situs of Statutory Construction - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Supreme Court of the Philippines, exercising its constitutional judicial power under Article VIII of the Constitution, serves as the ultimate situs and final arbiter for interpreting and construing the disputed provision.
* B. Purpose of Statutory Construction - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The purpose of construction is to discover and effectuate the true legislative intent and will of the lawmaking body, preventing injustice and ensuring the statute operates harmoniously.
* C. When to Construe - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court reaffirmed the cardinal threshold rule: Construction and interpretation are proper only where the statute is ambiguous, doubtful, or susceptible of multiple meanings. Where the law is clear, no construction is warranted.
* D. Ambiguity or Vagueness - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The statutory language was clear and unambiguous; the Court declined to find ambiguity where none existed in the text.
* E. Verba Legis Non Est Recedendum - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court adhered strictly to the literal wording of the statute, holding that from the words of a statute there should be no departure.
* F. Absoluta Sententia Expositore Non Indigent - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court held that when the language of the law is clear and unequivocal, it requires no interpreter and leaves no room for judicial construction.
* G. Legislative Intent - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court analyzed the true intent and policy of the lawmaking authority, ensuring that the statutory purpose is preserved and not defeated by erroneous administrative or private interpretations.
* H. Strict or Liberal Construction - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The Court applied standard ordinary rules of statutory interpretation without resorting to special strict or liberal construction doctrines.
* I. Prospective or Retrospective Construction - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The case involved only the contemporaneous, prospective application of the governing law.
* J. Statutory Construction vs. Judicial Legislation - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court underscored the constitutional boundary of judicial power: Courts are empowered solely to interpret (jus dicere), not to enact or amend laws (jus dare). Supplying unwritten terms or expanding clear prohibitions constitutes prohibited judicial legislation.
* K. Legis Interpretatio Legis Vim Obtinet - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. Under Article 8 of the Civil Code, the Supreme Court's authoritative interpretation of the statute forms part of the legal system of the Philippines and acquires the force of law (*legis interpretatio legis vim obtinet*).
* L. Extent and Limits of the Power to Construe - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court respected the constitutional limits of its authority by applying the law as written and refusing to invade the legislative domain under the guise of equity or expediency.

VIII. OTHER CANONS OR METHODS OF CONSTRUCTION
* Canon/Method: • Verba legis non est recedendum (plain text on numerical fractions)
• Judicis est ius dicere non dare (judges declare law, not make it)
* How Applied: Applied as corroborative canons to reinforce the primary rule of construction.
* Effect on Interpretation: Harmonized the statute and prevented an absurd or unjust result.

IX. THE CONSTRUCTION MADE BY THE COURT
* Summary Formula: The Court construed the disputed statutory provisions in Baking v. Director of Prisons to mean and apply strictly in accordance with Dura lex, sed lex, holding that No. The text of the law explicitly said 'one-half'. Even if the rule was harsh and unfair, judges cannot change the math written in the statute. The duty of the.
* Principal Reasoning: The law said 'one-half'. You cannot read 'one-half' and pretend it says 'full credit'. Even though the Justices sympathized with the prisoner, they followed their judicial duty to apply the statute as enacted, while urging Congress to amend the law (which Congress did in RA 6127).

X. STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION OR JUDICIAL LEGISLATION?
[X] Proper statutory construction    [ ] Judicial legislation    [ ] Debatable
Explanation: The Supreme Court acted within the legitimate bounds of its constitutional power to interpret the law (jus dicere). By applying Dura lex, sed lex, the Court faithfully effectuated the legislative intent without creating new rules, modifying clear statutory text, or usurping congressional prerogative. The ruling reinforces the separation of powers and the doctrine that courts must enforce the law as enacted.

XI. DOCTRINE:
Judicial Restraint on Explicit Statutory Quantities: Where the statute specifies an exact numerical ratio or fraction, courts must enforce it strictly under Dura Lex Sed Lex; humanitarian adjustments belong exclusively to the legislature.

XII. ONE-SENTENCE TAKEAWAY:
This case is important in Statutory Construction because Judges cannot change numbers in a law; if the statute says 'half', judges must apply 'half' until Congress passes a new law.

===============================================================================================
CASE #19: METROPOLITAN MANILA DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY (MMDA) V. DANTE O. GARIN
Citation: G.R. No. 130230 | April 15, 2005 | 456 SCRA 176 | En Banc | Ponente: J. Chico-Nazario
===============================================================================================

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LAYER A: ALAC DIGEST, RECITATION SCRIPT & STATCON ANALYSIS
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I. PARTIES & PROCEDURAL BACKGROUND
* Plaintiff/Petitioner: Metropolitan Manila Development Authority (MMDA) (Petitioner)
* Defendant/Respondent: Dante O. Garin (Respondent)
* Originating Court: Regional Trial Court of Parañaque City (Branch 258) / Supreme Court En Banc
* Nature of the Action: Petition for Review on Certiorari under Rule 45 concerning whether MMDA has statutory police power under Republic Act No. 7924 to confiscate and suspend driver's licenses.

II. WHOLE FACTS
Atty. Dante Garin was flagged down by an MMDA traffic enforcer in Parañaque City for illegal parking. The enforcer confiscated Garin's driver's license and issued a Traffic Violation Receipt (TVR) pursuant to MMDA Memorandum Circular No. 92-005. Garin filed an injunction suit before the RTC, arguing that MMDA possesses no police power or legislative authority under its charter (R.A. No. 7924) to confiscate driver's licenses. The MMDA argued that Section 5(f) of RA 7924, which empowers it to 'install and administer a single ticketing system and organize administrative suspensions,' implies the power to confiscate licenses.

Plain Meaning Summary of Facts:
An MMDA traffic enforcer confiscated a lawyer's driver's license for illegal parking. The lawyer sued, claiming MMDA has no legal power to seize licenses because it is not a police force and has no lawmaking powers. The Supreme Court examined the Congressional Deliberations (Legislative Debates) of RA 7924 as an extrinsic aid and confirmed that Congress explicitly stripped MMDA of police power and legislative authority, turning it into an administrative coordination agency only. Therefore, MMDA cannot confiscate driver's licenses without a local ordinance.

III. THE LEGAL ISSUE
Whether or not the MMDA has the statutory authority under R.A. No. 7924 to confiscate driver's licenses, using Legislative Debates and Congressional Journals as Extrinsic Aids.

[HOW TO TACKLE IN RECITATION: THE LEGAL ISSUE]
* Plain Meaning: Does the MMDA have the legal power to confiscate driver's licenses under its charter, when checked against Congressional debates?
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the issue is whether MMDA possesses police power to confiscate driver's licenses under RA 7924, testing Legislative Debates and Congressional Records as Extrinsic Aids to Construction and Potestas Delegata Non Delegari Potest."

IV. ALAC DIGEST

[A] ANSWER:
NO. The MMDA has NO STATUTORY AUTHORITY to confiscate driver's licenses. Extrinsic aids—specifically the LEGISLATIVE DEBATES on R.A. No. 7924—reveal that Congress explicitly intended to create the MMDA as an administrative coordinating body, NOT a political police power agency.

* Plain Meaning: No. The transcripts of the Senate and House debates prove that lawmakers deliberately refused to give MMDA police power. MMDA is an administrative agency that coordinates services; it cannot take away licenses unless authorized by a valid local municipal ordinance.
* Recitation Script: "No, Sir/Ma'am. The Supreme Court held that under Legislative Debates as an Extrinsic Aid, Congress explicitly stripped MMDA of police power and legislative authority. As affirmed in MMDA v. Bel-Air Village, MMDA cannot confiscate driver's licenses under RA 7924 without a specific delegation from local sanggunians."

[L] LEGAL BASIS:
* 1. Republic Act No. 7924 (MMDA Charter): Creates the MMDA as an administrative planning and coordinating agency for basic services.
* 2. Legislative Debates and Congressional Records as Extrinsic Aids: Statements and deliberations of legislators during floor debates reveal the definitive legislative intent regarding agency powers.
* 3. Potestas Delegata Non Delegari Potest (a delegated power cannot be delegated): Police power is inherently legislative; an administrative agency cannot assume police power by implication.
* Plain Meaning: RA 7924, Legislative Debates as Extrinsic Aids, and Potestas Delegata Non Delegari Potest.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the legal basis is RA 7924, interpreted through Legislative Debates and Committee Records as Extrinsic Aids under the doctrine of Potestas delegata non delegari potest."

[A] ANALYSIS / APPLICATION:
* 1. Legislative Intent from Floor Debates: The transcripts of the Senate deliberations (speeches of Senators Macapagal-Arroyo, Tolentino, and Biazon) clearly show that Congress explicitly rejected giving MMDA police power or ordinance-making authority.
* 2. Administrative vs. Police Authority: MMDA is limited to planning, monitoring, and coordinating functions. Regulating and seizing driver's licenses is an exercise of police power belonging exclusively to the LTO and local government units under the Local Government Code.
* 3. Subordination of Administrative Regulations: MMDA circulars ordering automatic license confiscation are ultra vires and void because they exceed the statutory authority conferred by Congress.
* Plain Meaning: When reading the official transcripts of the Senate debates on RA 7924, the Senators clearly agreed: 'MMDA is not a local government; it has no police power.' Because the legislative debates show Congress deliberately withheld the power to seize licenses, the MMDA cannot give itself that power through internal circulars.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the Court utilized the Congressional Records as an extrinsic aid. The debates prove that Congress deliberately designed MMDA without police power. Under the principle that administrative powers cannot exceed statutory grants, MMDA's license confiscation was declared ultra vires."

[C] CONCLUSION:
The Supreme Court AFFIRMED the trial court decision, ENJOINED the MMDA from confiscating driver's licenses without statutory or ordinance authority, and ORDERED the return of Garin's license.

* Plain Meaning: The lawyer won and MMDA was prohibited from confiscating driver's licenses without an ordinance.
* Recitation Script: "The Supreme Court prohibited MMDA from confiscating driver's licenses, using Congressional debates as an extrinsic aid to define the limits of agency power."

V. RELATION TO STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION
* Syllabus Topic Alignment:
  IV. Aids to Construction -> B. Extrinsic Aids -> a. Policy & b. Purpose of the Law (RA 7924 / Absence of Police Power to Confiscate Driver's Licenses)

* Governing Latin Maxims with English Translations:
  1. Potestas delegata non delegari potest (a delegated power cannot be delegated)
     - Literal Translation: Delegated power cannot be further delegated.
     - StatCon Application: Police power is vested in the Legislature and local sanggunians; MMDA cannot assume it by implication.
  2. Optima statuti interpretatrix est ipsum statutum (the best interpreter of a statute is the statute itself)
     - Literal Translation: The best interpreter of a statute is the statute itself.
     - StatCon Application: Congressional records and debates explain the true boundaries of the statutory enactment.
  3. Ultra vires non potest (an act beyond powers is void)
     - Literal Translation: Beyond powers cannot stand.
     - StatCon Application: Administrative circulars issued without statutory authorization are null and void.

* Case Violation in StatCon: MMDA attempted to arrogate inherent police powers through administrative circulars in direct defiance of the legislative intent recorded in Congressional debates.
* Doctrinal Execution: Legislative Debates as Extrinsic Aids: The record of floor debates in Congress is a recognized extrinsic aid to determine the precise nature, scope, and limitations of powers granted to administrative agencies.

* Plain StatCon Meaning: If you want to know what a law means, read what the Senators and Congressmen said when they debated and voted on the bill.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, MMDA v. Garin is the leading authority on using Congressional Floor Debates as an extrinsic aid to establish that MMDA lacks police power to seize licenses."

LAYER B: STANDARDIZED CASE DIGEST (STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION FORMAT)

CASE #20: COMMISSIONER OF INTERNAL REVENUE V. SM PRIME HOLDINGS, INC. AND FIRST ASIA REALTY DEVELOPMENT CORP.
Citation: G.R. No. 183505 | February 26, 2010 | 613 SCRA 774 | First Division | Ponente: J. Del Castillo
===============================================================================================

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LAYER A: ALAC DIGEST, RECITATION SCRIPT & STATCON ANALYSIS
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I. PARTIES & PROCEDURAL BACKGROUND
* Plaintiff/Petitioner: Commissioner of Internal Revenue (Petitioner)
* Defendant/Respondent: SM Prime Holdings, Inc. and First Asia Realty Development Corp. (Respondents)
* Originating Court: Court of Tax Appeals (En Banc) / Supreme Court
* Nature of the Action: Petition for Review on Certiorari under Rule 45 concerning whether cinema ticket sales are subject to Value-Added Tax (VAT) under Section 108 of the National Internal Revenue Code of 1997 and Revenue Memorandum Circular (RMC) No. 28-2001.

II. WHOLE FACTS
The BIR issued Revenue Memorandum Circular (RMC) No. 28-2001 declaring that gross receipts from cinema ticket sales and film exhibition are subject to 10% Value-Added Tax (VAT) under Section 108 of the NIRC of 1997 as 'services of the same kind'. Based on this circular, the CIR assessed SM Prime Holdings and First Asia Realty for deficiency VAT on their movie theater admissions. SM Prime protested, arguing that: (1) Section 108 does not include cinema exhibition in its enumeration of taxable services; (2) under the Local Government Code (Section 140), cinema admissions are subject to local amusement tax, and legislative history shows Congress never intended to impose national VAT on movie tickets; and (3) the BIR cannot expand the Tax Code via an administrative circular.

Plain Meaning Summary of Facts:
The BIR issued a memorandum circular saying all movie ticket sales in mall cinemas must pay a 10% national VAT. SM Prime challenged the tax, pointing out that movie theaters already pay local amusement taxes to cities, and that the Tax Code never mentioned cinema tickets under VAT. The Supreme Court investigated the Legislative History of the Tax Code as an extrinsic aid and confirmed that Congress deliberately exempted cinema admissions from national VAT to avoid double taxation with local amusement taxes. The BIR circular was declared illegal.

III. THE LEGAL ISSUE
Whether or not gross receipts from cinema theater admissions are subject to Value-Added Tax (VAT) under Section 108 of the NIRC, using Legislative History and Contemporaneous Construction as Extrinsic Aids.

[HOW TO TACKLE IN RECITATION: THE LEGAL ISSUE]
* Plain Meaning: Are cinema ticket sales subject to national VAT, when examined through the legislative history of the Tax Code?
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the issue is whether cinema admissions are subject to VAT under Section 108 NIRC, testing Legislative History as an Extrinsic Aid to Construction and the rule that Administrative Regulations cannot expand the Tax Code (Strictissimi Juris)."

IV. ALAC DIGEST

[A] ANSWER:
NO. Cinema ticket admissions are NOT subject to Value-Added Tax (VAT). EXTRINSIC AIDS—specifically the LEGISLATIVE HISTORY of the NIRC and VAT enactments—demonstrate that Congress deliberately excluded cinema operators from VAT coverage to leave the taxation of cinemas exclusively to local government amusement taxes.

* Plain Meaning: No. The legislative history shows Congress deliberately left out cinema tickets from VAT because local cities already tax movie tickets with an amusement tax. The BIR cannot invent a new VAT on movies through an administrative circular.
* Recitation Script: "No, Sir/Ma'am. The Supreme Court held that under Legislative History as an Extrinsic Aid, Congress consistently excluded motion picture exhibition from the VAT system. Under Strictissimi Juris, the BIR cannot expand Section 108 through RMC No. 28-2001, rendering the circular null and void."

[L] LEGAL BASIS:
* 1. Section 108, National Internal Revenue Code of 1997: Enumerates transactions and services subject to VAT, omitting cinema and theatrical admissions.
* 2. Legislative History and Prior Enactments as Extrinsic Aids: The evolution of VAT statutes from E.O. 273 to R.A. 7716 and R.A. 8424 shows the continuous legislative intent to exclude cinema admissions.
* 3. Strictissimi Juris (of the strictest interpretation) in Tax Imposition: Taxes cannot be imposed by administrative implication; statutes are strictly construed against the taxing authority.
* Plain Meaning: Section 108 NIRC, Legislative History as Extrinsic Aid, and Strictissimi Juris.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the legal basis is Section 108 NIRC, interpreted through Legislative History as an Extrinsic Aid and the bedrock canon Strictissimi Juris in taxation."

[A] ANALYSIS / APPLICATION:
* 1. Legislative Evolution of VAT Laws: From the original VAT law (E.O. No. 273) through the Expanded VAT (R.A. No. 7716) and the 1997 Tax Code, cinema exhibition was never included in the VAT base. In fact, proposed amendments to include movie theaters were repeatedly rejected by Congress.
* 2. Harmonization with Local Government Code: Under Section 140 LGC, local governments are granted the exclusive power to levy amusement taxes on cinemas. Congress avoided double taxation by keeping cinema admissions outside the national VAT system.
* 3. Administrative Regulations Cannot Legislate: RMC No. 28-2001 was an unauthorized exercise of administrative legislation that attempted to tax a service that the Legislature deliberately excluded.
* Plain Meaning: The Court traced the entire history of tax laws passed by Congress over 20 years. Every time someone proposed adding movie tickets to the VAT law, Congress voted NO. When the law is traced historically, the legislative intent is crystal clear: only cities collect amusement tax on movies, and the BIR cannot change the law with a circular.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the Court applied Legislative History as an extrinsic aid. The legislative trail shows continuous, deliberate congressional refusal to include cinema exhibitions in VAT. The Court struck down the BIR circular for usurping the legislative power of Congress."

[C] CONCLUSION:
The Supreme Court DENIED the CIR's petition, AFFIRMED the Court of Tax Appeals, and DECLARED RMC No. 28-2001 NULL AND VOID for imposing an unauthorized tax on cinema admissions.

* Plain Meaning: SM Prime won; the cinema VAT assessment and BIR circular were declared illegal and void.
* Recitation Script: "The Supreme Court voided the BIR circular and cancelled the VAT assessment on cinema tickets under the doctrine of Legislative History."

V. RELATION TO STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION
* Syllabus Topic Alignment:
  IV. Aids to Construction -> B. Extrinsic Aids -> c. Legislative History & e. Contemporaneous Construction (Movie Theaters Not Subject to VAT)

* Governing Latin Maxims with English Translations:
  1. Strictissimi juris (of the strictest interpretation)
     - Literal Translation: Of the strictest law.
     - StatCon Application: Tax statutes are construed strictly against the government; taxes cannot be created by administrative regulation.
  2. Optima statuti interpretatrix est ipsum statutum (the best interpreter of a statute is the statute itself)
     - Literal Translation: The best interpreter of a statute is the statute itself.
     - StatCon Application: Legislative history and amendments reveal the true statutory boundaries of the VAT law.
  3. Potestas delegata non delegari potest (a delegated power cannot be delegated)
     - Literal Translation: Delegated power cannot be further delegated.
     - StatCon Application: The BIR cannot usurp the constitutional power of Congress to impose new taxes.

* Case Violation in StatCon: The BIR issued an administrative circular that created a new tax on cinema tickets in direct violation of the legislative history of the Tax Code.
* Doctrinal Execution: Legislative History as a Constraint on Executive Agencies: The legislative history and evolution of a statute serve as an authoritative extrinsic aid to prevent administrative agencies from expanding tax liabilities beyond statutory boundaries.

* Plain StatCon Meaning: Tracing the history of how a law was changed over the years proves what Congress wanted, preventing tax agencies from inventing new taxes.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, CIR v. SM Prime Holdings is the leading case on Legislative History as an extrinsic aid to invalidate unauthorized administrative tax regulations."

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LAYER B: STANDARDIZED CASE DIGEST (STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION FORMAT)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Case Title: Commissioner of Internal Revenue v. SM Prime Holdings, Inc. and First Asia Realty Development Corp.
G.R. No.:   G.R. No. 183505
Date:       February 26, 2010
Ponente:    First Division

I. FACTS OF THE CASE
The BIR issued Revenue Memorandum Circular (RMC) No. 28-2001 declaring that gross receipts from cinema ticket sales and film exhibition are subject to 10% Value-Added Tax (VAT) under Section 108 of the NIRC of 1997 as 'services of the same kind'. Based on this circular, the CIR assessed SM Prime Holdings and First Asia Realty for deficiency VAT on their movie theater admissions. SM Prime protested, arguing that: (1) Section 108 does not include cinema exhibition in its enumeration of taxable services; (2) under the Local Government Code (Section 140), cinema admissions are subject to local amusement tax, and legislative history shows Congress never intended to impose national VAT on movie tickets; and (3) the BIR cannot expand the Tax Code via an administrative circular.

II. FACTS RELEVANT TO STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION
* Provision Relevant: 1. Section 108, National Internal Revenue Code of 1997: Enumerates transactions and services subject to VAT, omitting cinema and theatrical admissions.
2. Legislative History and Prior Enactments as Extrinsic Aids: The evolution of VAT statutes from E.O. 273 to R.A. 7716 and R.A. 8424 shows the continuous legislative intent to exclude cinema admissions.
* Circumstance Making Construction Necessary: The BIR issued an administrative circular that created a new tax on cinema tickets in direct violation of the legislative history of the Tax Code.
* Competing Interpretations: Petitioner argued for a favorable interpretation based on statutory intent, while Respondent contended for a literal or restrictive application.

III. ISSUES
A. General Legal Issues: Whether or not gross receipts from cinema theater admissions are subject to Value-Added Tax (VAT) under Section 108 of the NIRC, using Legislative History and Contemporaneous Construction as Extrinsic Aids.
B. Statutory-Construction Issue/s: Whether the subject statutory act, classification, or terminology under the governing statute/rule means, includes, or applies to the factual controversy in Commissioner of Internal Revenue v. SM Prime Holdings, Inc. and First Asia Realty Development Corp., under the canon of Strictissimi juris.

IV. PROVISION/S OF LAW CONSTRUED
* Provision: 1. Section 108, National Internal Revenue Code of 1997: Enumerates transactions and services subject to VAT, omitting cinema and theatrical admissions.
2. Legislative History and Prior Enactments as Extrinsic Aids: The evolution of VAT statutes from E.O. 273 to R.A. 7716 and R.A. 8424 shows the continuous legislative intent to exclude cinema admissions.
* Interpretive Problem: Determining the precise legislative intent and boundary of the statutory terms in light of: The BIR issued an administrative circular that created a new tax on cinema tickets in direct violation of the legislativ...

V. RULING OF THE SUPREME COURT
A. General Ruling: The Supreme Court DENIED the CIR's petition, AFFIRMED the Court of Tax Appeals, and DECLARED RMC No. 28-2001 NULL AND VOID for imposing an unauthorized tax on cinema admissions.
B. Ruling on Statutory-Construction Issue: NO. Cinema ticket admissions are NOT subject to Value-Added Tax (VAT). EXTRINSIC AIDS—specifically the LEGISLATIVE HISTORY of the NIRC and VAT enactments—demonstrate that Congress deliberately excluded cinema operators from VAT coverage to leave the taxation of cinemas exclusively to local government amusement taxes.

VI. HOW THE COURT CONSTRUED THE LAW
A. Why Was Construction Necessary? Ambiguity in the application and scope of the statute: The BIR issued an administrative circular that created a new tax on cinema tickets in direct violation of the legislative history of the Tax.
B. Purpose of Construction: To ascertain and give full effect to the true intent, spirit, and purpose of the lawmaking body.
C. Method/Canon Applied: Strictissimi juris
   - How Applied: Legislative History as a Constraint on Executive Agencies: The legislative history and evolution of a statute serve as an authoritative extrinsic aid to prevent administrative agencies from expanding tax liabilities beyond statutory boundaries.
   - Result: No. The legislative history shows Congress deliberately left out cinema tickets from VAT because local cities already tax movie tickets with an amusement tax. The BIR cannot invent a new VAT on movies through an administrative circular.
D. Other Sources Used: Ordinary meaning of words, statute considered as a whole, legislative history, and established Supreme Court jurisprudence.

VII. RELEVANT STATUTORY-CONSTRUCTION CONCEPTS (A–L EVALUATION)
* A. Situs of Statutory Construction - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Supreme Court of the Philippines, exercising its constitutional judicial power under Article VIII of the Constitution, serves as the ultimate situs and final arbiter for interpreting and construing the disputed provision.
* B. Purpose of Statutory Construction - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The purpose of construction is to discover and effectuate the true legislative intent and will of the lawmaking body, preventing injustice and ensuring the statute operates harmoniously.
* C. When to Construe - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. Construction became imperative because the text contained an ambiguity, competing interpretations, or conflicting statutory provisions that required judicial determination of legislative intent.
* D. Ambiguity or Vagueness - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The controversy centered on the ambiguity, silence, or conflicting scope of the statutory language, which the Court resolved by examining legislative intent and governing maxims.
* E. Verba Legis Non Est Recedendum - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The Court looked beyond literal words to discover the underlying legislative spirit, context, and intent.
* F. Absoluta Sententia Expositore Non Indigent - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The statutory language required expository interpretation due to textual gaps, broad phrasing, or conflicting provisions.
* G. Legislative Intent - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court analyzed the true intent and policy of the lawmaking authority, ensuring that the statutory purpose is preserved and not defeated by erroneous administrative or private interpretations.
* H. Strict or Liberal Construction - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The Court applied standard ordinary rules of statutory interpretation without resorting to special strict or liberal construction doctrines.
* I. Prospective or Retrospective Construction - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The case involved only the contemporaneous, prospective application of the governing law.
* J. Statutory Construction vs. Judicial Legislation - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court underscored the constitutional boundary of judicial power: Courts are empowered solely to interpret (jus dicere), not to enact or amend laws (jus dare). Supplying unwritten terms or expanding clear prohibitions constitutes prohibited judicial legislation.
* K. Legis Interpretatio Legis Vim Obtinet - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. Under Article 8 of the Civil Code, the Supreme Court's authoritative interpretation of the statute forms part of the legal system of the Philippines and acquires the force of law (*legis interpretatio legis vim obtinet*).
* L. Extent and Limits of the Power to Construe - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court respected the constitutional limits of its authority by applying the law as written and refusing to invade the legislative domain under the guise of equity or expediency.

VIII. OTHER CANONS OR METHODS OF CONSTRUCTION
* Canon/Method: • Optima statuti interpretatrix (legislative history as guide)
• Potestas delegata non delegari potest (administrative agencies cannot legislate)
* How Applied: Applied as corroborative canons to reinforce the primary rule of construction.
* Effect on Interpretation: Harmonized the statute and prevented an absurd or unjust result.

IX. THE CONSTRUCTION MADE BY THE COURT
* Summary Formula: The Court construed the disputed statutory provisions in Commissioner of Internal Revenue v. SM Prime Holdings, Inc. and First Asia Realty Development Corp. to mean and apply strictly in accordance with Strictissimi juris, holding that No. The legislative history shows Congress deliberately left out cinema tickets from VAT because local cities already tax movie tickets with an amusement tax. T.
* Principal Reasoning: The Court traced the entire history of tax laws passed by Congress over 20 years. Every time someone proposed adding movie tickets to the VAT law, Congress voted NO. When the law is traced historically, the legislative intent is crystal clear: only cities collect amusement tax on movies, and the BIR cannot change the law with a circular.

X. STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION OR JUDICIAL LEGISLATION?
[X] Proper statutory construction    [ ] Judicial legislation    [ ] Debatable
Explanation: The Supreme Court acted within the legitimate bounds of its constitutional power to interpret the law (jus dicere). By applying Strictissimi juris, the Court faithfully effectuated the legislative intent without creating new rules, modifying clear statutory text, or usurping congressional prerogative. The ruling reinforces the separation of powers and the doctrine that courts must enforce the law as enacted.

XI. DOCTRINE:
Legislative History as a Constraint on Executive Agencies: The legislative history and evolution of a statute serve as an authoritative extrinsic aid to prevent administrative agencies from expanding tax liabilities beyond statutory boundaries.

XII. ONE-SENTENCE TAKEAWAY:
This case is important in Statutory Construction because Tracing the history of how a law was changed over the years proves what Congress wanted, preventing tax agencies from inventing new taxes.

===============================================================================================
CASE #21: LAXAMANA V. BALTAZAR
Citation: G.R. No. L-5955 | September 19, 1952 | 92 Phil. 32 | En Banc | Ponente: J. Bengzon
===============================================================================================

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LAYER A: ALAC DIGEST, RECITATION SCRIPT & STATCON ANALYSIS
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I. PARTIES & PROCEDURAL BACKGROUND
* Plaintiff/Petitioner: Jose Laxamana (Petitioner)
* Defendant/Respondent: Flaviano Baltazar (Respondent)
* Originating Court: Supreme Court En Banc (Original Action for Quo Warranto)
* Nature of the Action: Petition for Quo Warranto concerning whether the Vice-Mayor or a Provincial Governor's appointee succeeds as Mayor during the temporary suspension of the Municipal Mayor under Section 2195 of the Revised Administrative Code.

II. WHOLE FACTS
The Municipal Mayor of Sexmoan, Pampanga was suspended from office pending administrative charges. The Provincial Governor appointed Flaviano Baltazar to take over as Mayor. The elected Vice-Mayor, Jose Laxamana, contested the appointment and claimed the right to assume the office of Mayor pursuant to Section 2195 of the Revised Administrative Code, which provided that 'upon the occasion of the absence, suspension, or other temporary disability of the mayor, his duties shall be discharged by the vice-mayor.' Baltazar argued that Section 21(a) of the Revised Election Code gave the Governor the general power to fill vacancies in municipal offices.

Plain Meaning Summary of Facts:
The mayor of a town was suspended. The Provincial Governor appointed an outsider to act as mayor. The elected Vice-Mayor filed a lawsuit, arguing that the Administrative Code explicitly says that when the mayor is suspended, the vice-mayor takes over the duties. The outsider argued that the Election Code gave the Governor the right to fill vacancies. The Supreme Court investigated the legislative history of local government succession and ruled that Section 2195 specifically provides for automatic temporary succession by the vice-mayor, which prevails over the general vacancy power of the Governor.

III. THE LEGAL ISSUE
Whether or not the Vice-Mayor automatically assumes the office of Mayor during the temporary suspension of the incumbent Mayor, using Legislative History as an Extrinsic Aid.

[HOW TO TACKLE IN RECITATION: THE LEGAL ISSUE]
* Plain Meaning: Does the Vice-Mayor automatically take over when the Mayor is suspended, or can the Governor appoint someone else?
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the issue is whether the Vice-Mayor succeeds to the office during temporary suspension of the Mayor under Section 2195 of the Administrative Code, testing Legislative History and Generalia Specialibus Non Derogant."

IV. ALAC DIGEST

[A] ANSWER:
YES. The VICE-MAYOR automatically assumes the duties of the Mayor during the Mayor's temporary suspension. Section 2195 of the Revised Administrative Code is a SPECIAL PROVISION on temporary disability that prevails over the general vacancy rules of the Election Code.

* Plain Meaning: Yes. The law specifically says the Vice-Mayor takes over when the Mayor is suspended. The Governor's general power to fill permanent vacancies cannot be used to bypass the elected Vice-Mayor during temporary suspensions.
* Recitation Script: "Yes, Sir/Ma'am. The Supreme Court held that under Legislative History as an Extrinsic Aid and Generalia specialibus non derogant, Section 2195 of the Revised Administrative Code specifically mandates automatic temporary succession by the Vice-Mayor. The Governor has no power to appoint a substitute where the law provides an automatic successor."

[L] LEGAL BASIS:
* 1. Section 2195, Revised Administrative Code: Specific provision mandating that upon the absence, suspension, or temporary disability of the mayor, his duties shall be discharged by the vice-mayor.
* 2. Section 21(a), Revised Election Code: General provision governing the filling of vacancies in municipal offices.
* 3. Generalia Specialibus Non Derogant (general provisions yield to special provisions): The specific automatic succession rule for temporary disability controls over general vacancy filling.
* 4. Legislative History as an Extrinsic Aid: The historical evolution of municipal administration laws confirms the statutory policy of succession through the vice-mayor.
* Plain Meaning: Section 2195 RAC, Section 21(a) Election Code, Generalia Specialibus Non Derogant, and Legislative History.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the legal basis is Section 2195 of the Revised Administrative Code, applied under Generalia specialibus non derogant (general laws yield to special provisions) and Legislative History as an Extrinsic Aid."

[A] ANALYSIS / APPLICATION:
* 1. Legislative Evolution of Municipal Succession: The historical purpose of creating the office of Vice-Mayor was precisely to have a readily available, democratically elected substitute to assume executive duties instantly during the mayor's temporary absence or disability.
* 2. Distinction Between Permanent Vacancy and Temporary Disability: Section 21(a) of the Election Code governs permanent vacancies (death, permanent resignation, removal). Section 2195 of the Administrative Code specifically governs temporary suspensions.
* 3. Democratic Will of the Electorate: The electorate elected the Vice-Mayor with the expectation that he would step into the Mayor's shoes when needed; executive appointment of a political favorite violates the statutory scheme.
* Plain Meaning: Why do people elect a Vice-Mayor? To take over when the Mayor is sick, away, or suspended! If the Governor could appoint a political ally whenever the Mayor is suspended, the office of Vice-Mayor would be meaningless. The specific succession law controls.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the Court examined the legislative history of municipal governance. The specific text of Section 2195 RAC guarantees automatic succession by the Vice-Mayor during temporary suspensions. Under Generalia Specialibus Non Derogant, this specific rule defeats general gubernatorial vacancy powers."

[C] CONCLUSION:
The Supreme Court GRANTED the petition for Quo Warranto, OUSTED respondent Baltazar, and DECLARED petitioner Vice-Mayor Jose Laxamana entitled to discharge the duties of Mayor.

* Plain Meaning: The Vice-Mayor won and was declared the rightful acting Mayor.
* Recitation Script: "The Supreme Court ousted the Governor's appointee and declared the Vice-Mayor the lawful acting Mayor under Section 2195 RAC."

V. RELATION TO STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION
* Syllabus Topic Alignment:
  IV. Aids to Construction -> B. Extrinsic Aids -> c. Legislative History & Legislative Evolution (RAC Sec. 2195 / Vice-Mayor Succession)

* Governing Latin Maxims with English Translations:
  1. Generalia specialibus non derogant (general things do not derogate from special things)
     - Literal Translation: General words do not derogate from special provisions.
     - StatCon Application: The specific temporary succession rule in Section 2195 RAC prevails over general vacancy provisions in the Election Code.
  2. Ratio legis est anima legis (the reason of the law is the soul of the law)
     - Literal Translation: The reason of the law is the soul of the law.
     - StatCon Application: The legislative reason for creating the vice-mayoralty is to ensure continuous democratic local executive succession.
  3. Salus populi est suprema lex (the welfare of the people is the supreme law)
     - Literal Translation: The welfare of the people is the supreme law.
     - StatCon Application: Respecting the democratic choice of the voters for Vice-Mayor preserves local government stability.

* Case Violation in StatCon: The Provincial Governor attempted to use a general vacancy statute to override the specific statutory succession mechanism enacted for the Vice-Mayor.
* Doctrinal Execution: Primacy of Specific Succession Provisions: Where a statute creates an automatic line of succession for temporary disability, executive appointing authorities cannot create artificial vacancies to install political appointees.

* Plain StatCon Meaning: A specific law saying 'the vice-mayor takes over when the mayor is suspended' beats a general law about appointing replacements.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, Laxamana v. Baltazar is the foundational jurisprudence harmonizing local government succession through Legislative History and Generalia specialibus non derogant."

===============================================================================================
CASE #22: ALVAREZ V. GUINGONA
Citation: G.R. No. 118303 | January 31, 1996 | 252 SCRA 695 | En Banc | Ponente: J. Hermosisima, Jr.
===============================================================================================

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LAYER A: ALAC DIGEST, RECITATION SCRIPT & STATCON ANALYSIS
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

I. PARTIES & PROCEDURAL BACKGROUND
* Plaintiff/Petitioner: Alvarez (Petitioner)
* Defendant/Respondent: Executive Secretary Teofisto Guingona, Jr. (Respondent)
* Originating Court: Supreme Court En Banc (Original Action for Prohibition)
* Nature of the Action: Petition for Prohibition challenging the constitutionality of Republic Act No. 7720 converting the Municipality of Santiago, Isabela into an independent component city under the Enrolled Bill Doctrine.

II. WHOLE FACTS
Petitioners challenged the validity of Republic Act No. 7720 (Charter of the City of Santiago), contending that the law did not originate exclusively in the House of Representatives as required by Article VI, Section 24 of the 1987 Constitution for local bills. Petitioners cited entries in the Congressional Journals showing that the Senate passed its own version before receiving the House bill. The respondents invoked the ENROLLED BILL DOCTRINE, showing that the final bill was duly signed by the Speaker of the House and the Senate President and approved by the President of the Philippines.

Plain Meaning Summary of Facts:
A group of citizens challenged the law that turned the town of Santiago, Isabela into a city, claiming the law was unconstitutional because the Senate started working on the bill before the House finished it, citing entries in the legislative journals. The government defended the law using the Enrolled Bill Doctrine, showing that the final official copy of the bill was formally signed by the Senate President, Speaker of the House, and President of the Philippines. The Supreme Court upheld the city charter, ruling that the Enrolled Bill is conclusive evidence of the regular passage of a statute.

III. THE LEGAL ISSUE
Whether or not the Enrolled Bill Doctrine prevails over Congressional Journals and records in establishing the regular constitutional enactment of a statute (R.A. No. 7720).

[HOW TO TACKLE IN RECITATION: THE LEGAL ISSUE]
* Plain Meaning: Does the officially signed copy of a law (Enrolled Bill) beat journal notes when someone challenges how the law was passed in Congress?
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the issue is whether RA 7720 is constitutional under the Enrolled Bill Doctrine, testing whether an Enrolled Bill is conclusive proof of regular enactment over conflicting entries in Congressional Journals."

IV. ALAC DIGEST

[A] ANSWER:
YES. The ENROLLED BILL DOCTRINE prevails. The signing of a bill by the Speaker of the House and the Senate President and its certification as having passed both Houses is CONCLUSIVE PROOF of its due enactment, which courts cannot question by inspecting legislative journals.

* Plain Meaning: Yes. When the heads of both Houses of Congress sign a law and send it to the President, that official paper (Enrolled Bill) is final and conclusive. Courts respect the separation of powers and will not go digging through legislative diary notes to second-guess Congress.
* Recitation Script: "Yes, Sir/Ma'am. The Supreme Court held that under the Enrolled Bill Doctrine and the principle of separation of powers, an enrolled bill signed by the Senate President and House Speaker carries absolute verity and is conclusive upon the courts as to the regular passage of the statute."

[L] LEGAL BASIS:
* 1. Article VI, Section 24 and 26, 1987 Constitution: Constitutional requirements for legislative enactment of local bills and enrolled bill certification.
* 2. The Enrolled Bill Doctrine: An enrolled bill signed by the leaders of Congress imports absolute verity and is conclusive of its due enactment.
* 3. Presumption of Regularity and Constitutionality: Official acts of the legislative and executive branches are presumed regular and valid under Omnia Praesumuntur Rite Esse Acta.
* Plain Meaning: Article VI 1987 Constitution, Enrolled Bill Doctrine, and Omnia Praesumuntur Rite Esse Acta.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the legal basis is Article VI of the Constitution, governed by the Enrolled Bill Doctrine and the presumption Omnia praesumuntur rite et solemniter esse acta (all acts are presumed to have been done regularly)."

[A] ANALYSIS / APPLICATION:
* 1. Conclusiveness of the Enrolled Bill: The enrolled copy of a bill, bearing the authentic signatures of the Speaker of the House and the President of the Senate, is the definitive, solemn declaration of the Legislature that the bill was regularly passed in compliance with constitutional procedures.
* 2. Judicial Non-Interference in Legislative Process: To permit courts to examine legislative journals and second-guess the accuracy of legislative certifications would violate the separation of powers and create constitutional instability.
* 3. Originating in the House: Article VI, Section 24 only requires that the legislative initiative for local bills begin in the House; the Senate has full constitutional power to propose or concur with amendments, including passing its own consolidated version.
* Plain Meaning: The Constitution requires checks and balances. When the Senate President and House Speaker sign an official document saying 'this bill was properly voted on and passed', the Supreme Court must respect that official word. Judges do not go through minute-by-minute journal notes to impeach the official signature of Congress.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, the Court applied the Enrolled Bill Doctrine. The authentic signatures of the legislative leaders import absolute verity. Under the separation of powers and Omnia Praesumuntur Rite Esse Acta, the enrolled bill is conclusive proof of constitutional enactment."

[C] CONCLUSION:
The Supreme Court DISMISSED the petition and UPHELD the constitutionality and validity of Republic Act No. 7720 creating the City of Santiago.

* Plain Meaning: The petition was dismissed and the city charter was declared completely valid.
* Recitation Script: "The Supreme Court upheld the constitutionality of RA 7720, reaffirming the conclusive authority of the Enrolled Bill Doctrine."

V. RELATION TO STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION
* Syllabus Topic Alignment:
  IV. Aids to Construction -> B. Extrinsic Aids -> c. Legislative History & Congressional Debates (RA 7720 / Internal Revenue Allotment for City Conversion)

* Governing Latin Maxims with English Translations:
  1. Omnia praesumuntur rite et solemniter esse acta donec probetur in contrarium (all acts are presumed to have been done regularly and with solemnity until the contrary is proved)
     - Literal Translation: All things are presumed to have been done regularly.
     - StatCon Application: The enrolled bill is conclusively presumed to have been enacted in accordance with all constitutional rules.
  2. Res judicata pro veritate accipitur (a matter adjudged is taken for truth)
     - Literal Translation: An official matter settled is accepted as truth.
     - StatCon Application: The solemn certification of legislative leaders carries conclusive verity.
  3. Potestas delegata non delegari potest (a delegated power cannot be delegated)
     - Literal Translation: Delegated power cannot be further delegated.
     - StatCon Application: The legislative power to certify bills belongs exclusively to the leaders of Congress.

* Case Violation in StatCon: The petitioners attempted to undermine the solemn certification of Congress by using selective journal entries to attack an enrolled bill.
* Doctrinal Execution: The Enrolled Bill Doctrine: An enrolled bill signed by the presiding officers of both Houses of Congress and approved by the President imports absolute verity and is conclusive upon the judiciary regarding constitutional compliance.

* Plain StatCon Meaning: The officially signed copy of a law by the leaders of Congress is the final, untouchable truth that the law was passed properly.
* Recitation Script: "Sir/Ma'am, Alvarez v. Guingona is the prime authority on the Enrolled Bill Doctrine: the enrolled copy of a statute prevails conclusively over legislative journals."

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
LAYER B: STANDARDIZED CASE DIGEST (STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION FORMAT)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Case Title: Alvarez v. Guingona
G.R. No.:   G.R. No. 118303
Date:       January 31, 1996
Ponente:    En Banc

I. FACTS OF THE CASE
Petitioners challenged the validity of Republic Act No. 7720 (Charter of the City of Santiago), contending that the law did not originate exclusively in the House of Representatives as required by Article VI, Section 24 of the 1987 Constitution for local bills. Petitioners cited entries in the Congressional Journals showing that the Senate passed its own version before receiving the House bill. The respondents invoked the ENROLLED BILL DOCTRINE, showing that the final bill was duly signed by the Speaker of the House and the Senate President and approved by the President of the Philippines.

II. FACTS RELEVANT TO STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION
* Provision Relevant: 1. Article VI, Section 24 and 26, 1987 Constitution: Constitutional requirements for legislative enactment of local bills and enrolled bill certification.
2. The Enrolled Bill Doctrine: An enrolled bill signed by the leaders of Congress imports absolute verity and is conclusive of its due enactment.
* Circumstance Making Construction Necessary: The petitioners attempted to undermine the solemn certification of Congress by using selective journal entries to attack an enrolled bill.
* Competing Interpretations: Petitioner argued for a favorable interpretation based on statutory intent, while Respondent contended for a literal or restrictive application.

III. ISSUES
A. General Legal Issues: Whether or not the Enrolled Bill Doctrine prevails over Congressional Journals and records in establishing the regular constitutional enactment of a statute (R.A. No. 7720).
B. Statutory-Construction Issue/s: Whether the subject statutory act, classification, or terminology under the governing statute/rule means, includes, or applies to the factual controversy in Alvarez v. Guingona, under the canon of Omnia praesumuntur rite et solemniter esse acta.

IV. PROVISION/S OF LAW CONSTRUED
* Provision: 1. Article VI, Section 24 and 26, 1987 Constitution: Constitutional requirements for legislative enactment of local bills and enrolled bill certification.
2. The Enrolled Bill Doctrine: An enrolled bill signed by the leaders of Congress imports absolute verity and is conclusive of its due enactment.
* Interpretive Problem: Determining the precise legislative intent and boundary of the statutory terms in light of: The petitioners attempted to undermine the solemn certification of Congress by using selective journal entries to attack...

V. RULING OF THE SUPREME COURT
A. General Ruling: The Supreme Court DISMISSED the petition and UPHELD the constitutionality and validity of Republic Act No. 7720 creating the City of Santiago.
B. Ruling on Statutory-Construction Issue: YES. The ENROLLED BILL DOCTRINE prevails. The signing of a bill by the Speaker of the House and the Senate President and its certification as having passed both Houses is CONCLUSIVE PROOF of its due enactment, which courts cannot question by inspecting legislative journals.

VI. HOW THE COURT CONSTRUED THE LAW
A. Why Was Construction Necessary? Ambiguity in the application and scope of the statute: The petitioners attempted to undermine the solemn certification of Congress by using selective journal entries to attack an enrolled bill..
B. Purpose of Construction: To ascertain and give full effect to the true intent, spirit, and purpose of the lawmaking body.
C. Method/Canon Applied: Omnia praesumuntur rite et solemniter esse acta
   - How Applied: The Enrolled Bill Doctrine: An enrolled bill signed by the presiding officers of both Houses of Congress and approved by the President imports absolute verity and is conclusive upon the judiciary regarding constitutional compliance.
   - Result: Yes. When the heads of both Houses of Congress sign a law and send it to the President, that official paper (Enrolled Bill) is final and conclusive. Courts respect the separation of powers and will not go digging through legislative diary notes to second-guess Congress.
D. Other Sources Used: Ordinary meaning of words, statute considered as a whole, legislative history, and established Supreme Court jurisprudence.

VII. RELEVANT STATUTORY-CONSTRUCTION CONCEPTS (A–L EVALUATION)
* A. Situs of Statutory Construction - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Supreme Court of the Philippines, exercising its constitutional judicial power under Article VIII of the Constitution, serves as the ultimate situs and final arbiter for interpreting and construing the disputed provision.
* B. Purpose of Statutory Construction - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The purpose of construction is to discover and effectuate the true legislative intent and will of the lawmaking body, preventing injustice and ensuring the statute operates harmoniously.
* C. When to Construe - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. Construction became imperative because the text contained an ambiguity, competing interpretations, or conflicting statutory provisions that required judicial determination of legislative intent.
* D. Ambiguity or Vagueness - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The controversy centered on the ambiguity, silence, or conflicting scope of the statutory language, which the Court resolved by examining legislative intent and governing maxims.
* E. Verba Legis Non Est Recedendum - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The Court looked beyond literal words to discover the underlying legislative spirit, context, and intent.
* F. Absoluta Sententia Expositore Non Indigent - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The statutory language required expository interpretation due to textual gaps, broad phrasing, or conflicting provisions.
* G. Legislative Intent - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court analyzed the true intent and policy of the lawmaking authority, ensuring that the statutory purpose is preserved and not defeated by erroneous administrative or private interpretations.
* H. Strict or Liberal Construction - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The Court applied standard ordinary rules of statutory interpretation without resorting to special strict or liberal construction doctrines.
* I. Prospective or Retrospective Construction - [NOT APPLICABLE]: Not Applicable. The case involved only the contemporaneous, prospective application of the governing law.
* J. Statutory Construction vs. Judicial Legislation - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court underscored the constitutional boundary of judicial power: Courts are empowered solely to interpret (jus dicere), not to enact or amend laws (jus dare). Supplying unwritten terms or expanding clear prohibitions constitutes prohibited judicial legislation.
* K. Legis Interpretatio Legis Vim Obtinet - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. Under Article 8 of the Civil Code, the Supreme Court's authoritative interpretation of the statute forms part of the legal system of the Philippines and acquires the force of law (*legis interpretatio legis vim obtinet*).
* L. Extent and Limits of the Power to Construe - [APPLICABLE]: Applicable. The Court respected the constitutional limits of its authority by applying the law as written and refusing to invade the legislative domain under the guise of equity or expediency.

VIII. OTHER CANONS OR METHODS OF CONSTRUCTION
* Canon/Method: • Res judicata pro veritate accipitur (conclusive verity of official acts)
• Separation of powers doctrine
* How Applied: Applied as corroborative canons to reinforce the primary rule of construction.
* Effect on Interpretation: Harmonized the statute and prevented an absurd or unjust result.

IX. THE CONSTRUCTION MADE BY THE COURT
* Summary Formula: The Court construed the disputed statutory provisions in Alvarez v. Guingona to mean and apply strictly in accordance with Omnia praesumuntur rite et solemniter esse acta, holding that Yes. When the heads of both Houses of Congress sign a law and send it to the President, that official paper (Enrolled Bill) is final and conclusive. Courts resp.
* Principal Reasoning: The Constitution requires checks and balances. When the Senate President and House Speaker sign an official document saying 'this bill was properly voted on and passed', the Supreme Court must respect that official word. Judges do not go through minute-by-minute journal notes to impeach the official signature of Congress.

X. STATUTORY CONSTRUCTION OR JUDICIAL LEGISLATION?
[X] Proper statutory construction    [ ] Judicial legislation    [ ] Debatable
Explanation: The Supreme Court acted within the legitimate bounds of its constitutional power to interpret the law (jus dicere). By applying Omnia praesumuntur rite et solemniter esse acta, the Court faithfully effectuated the legislative intent without creating new rules, modifying clear statutory text, or usurping congressional prerogative. The ruling reinforces the separation of powers and the doctrine that courts must enforce the law as enacted.

XI. DOCTRINE:
The Enrolled Bill Doctrine: An enrolled bill signed by the presiding officers of both Houses of Congress and approved by the President imports absolute verity and is conclusive upon the judiciary regarding constitutional compliance.

XII. ONE-SENTENCE TAKEAWAY:
This case is important in Statutory Construction because The officially signed copy of a law by the leaders of Congress is the final, untouchable truth that the law was passed properly.
